# R2067 - d'Affry to "B", The Hague, 17 January 1758 (no. 283): reading

Rests on: the contemporary clear copy in the Fagel register, Nationaal Archief 1.10.29 inv. 5187, scans 14R-15L
(margin "Dat: 17. Janvier 1758. A: à B:"), transcribed by WAINUT (21 Oct 2026, `wainut/register_clear.txt`) and checked
line by line against the scans. The register is the deciphered text of this letter: with this folder's key, 334 of the
434 keyed groups of `transcr/R2067.txt` stand at the register's word at that point (0.770; shuffled key values 0.133,
max 0.171; `wainut/align/R2067.tsv`), from the first group to the end. The reading therefore follows the register, in
the clerk's spelling (v3 verification, 23 Oct 2026); the keyed decode it replaces is `R2067.md`.

Two places where the clerk left dotted gaps are holes here: "ad[re]ssé" (15L line 1, "cr.ssé" after "ad-") and
"[?désignées]" (15L line 3, "des.r.es"). Where the figures and the register differ (59 groups): two-valued groups,
names (1022 = "as" of Astier, 94 = la Haye) and clerk's abbreviations.

## Reading

J'ai l'honneur de vous envoyer aujourdhui Copie chiffrée de ce que Mr. Astier m'a écrit. Il me paroissoit inutile de
tenter en Angleterre même quelque Operation d'Argent, qui venant à être decouverte, feroit croire nos besoins plus
grands qu'ils ne sont, et feroit augmenter les precautions: Malgré ces Precautions les Anglois placeront encore chez
nous lorsque l'Interêt pourra les tenter, et ils ont encore actuellement chez des Banquiers de ce Païs, et à
Bruxelles, des fonds qui n'ont pu trouver place dans nos Rentes Viagères. Je sais qu'il y a ici deux ou trois jeunes
Anglois, dont l'un s'appelle Macdonald: S'il sort effectivement de France, et qu'il soit interessant de savoir ou il
va, et ce qu'il fait, je tacherai de me mêtre en état de vous en rendre compte. Il nous est arrivé de Paris deux
Espagnols, dont l'un est Mr. de Lastiri, Secretaire d'Ambassade à Paris: Ils doivent passer au premier jour à
Londres: Les Lettres du 10. de Londres ne nous ont rien apporté de nouveau.

J'ai eu encore hier le Juif Boas: Il m'a dit que Mr. de Beckerschee du Comptoir de Pels, étoit à la Haye pour l'Argent
saisi à Osnabrug, dans lequel ce Comptoir est interessé: Il m'a demandé s'il étoit venu chez moi, je lui ai dit que
non: Il m'a repondu qu'il en êtoit êtonné: Je lui ai repliqué que je ne l'étois pas, puisque Mrs. Pels ne produisent
rien pour justifier l'usage de l'Argent, il étoit apparent qu'il regardoit nos Ennemis: Sur quoi Boas m'a dit, qu'il ne
croyoit pas qu'il fut destiné directement au payement de Leurs Troupes, mais qu'il croyoit qu'il étoit destiné et
ad[re]ssé à des Sujets de Sa Majesté Prussienne, et je crois effectivement que toutes les Caisses qui ne sont pas
[?désignées] dans la Requête, finiront par être declarées de bonne prise.
Translation

R2067_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/R2067_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 23 Oct 2026), with the same holes. -->

# R2067, d'Affry to "B", The Hague, 17 January 1758 (no. 283): English translation

I have the honour to send you today a ciphered copy of what Mr Astier wrote to me. It seemed to me useless to attempt
any money operation in England itself, which, if it came to be discovered, would make our needs appear greater than
they are and would cause the precautions to be increased. Despite these precautions the English will still invest with
us when the interest can tempt them, and they still have at present, with bankers of this country and at Brussels,
funds that could not find a place in our life annuities. I know that there are here two or three young Englishmen, one
of whom is called Macdonald. If he is really leaving France, and it is of interest to know where he is going and what
he is doing, I shall try to put myself in a position to give you an account of it. Two Spaniards have arrived here
from Paris, one of whom is Mr de Lastiri, Secretary of the Embassy at Paris. They are to go over to London any day now.
The letters of the 10th from London have brought us nothing new.

Yesterday I again had the Jew Boas. He told me that Mr de Beckerschee of the Pels counting-house was at The Hague about
the money seized at Osnabrück, in which that house has an interest. He asked me whether he had come to see me; I told
him no. He answered that he was surprised at it. I replied that I was not, since, as the Messrs Pels produce nothing to
justify the use of the money, it was apparent that it concerned our enemies. Whereupon Boas told me that he did not
believe it was intended directly for the payment of their troops, but that he believed it was intended for and
ad[dre]ssed to subjects of His Prussian Majesty; and I do indeed believe that all the chests that are not [?specified]
in the petition will end by being declared good prize.

Key: key_M.json, key_X.tsv
